### `8c758f80dbf1` — inkling cuda: K256 SF4 expert GEMM for prefill chunks (TR=128), bit-identical to k_moe_sf4_tc_tr128, 1.65x on the gate+up call

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_moe_fp4_sm120.cu` (+331/-1)

- Related decisions:
  - No direct matrix decision; accepted as upstream baseline.

**Original commit message**

```
Profile of a 4096-token prefill chunk on the RTX 5090: the expert GEMMs are 46% of the kernel time, at 15% of the FP4 tensor-core peak. Ablations in a standalone
bench (pf_moe_bench: synthetic experts, skewed top-8 routing, hashes of the outputs) and a load-only bench (pf_ldbench) show that the cp.async streams of the shipped
kernel alone take as long as the whole kernel (1.18 ms for gate+up): every warp-wide copy touches 16 cache lines and moves 32 of the 128 bytes of each (K128 stages).

k_moe_sf4_tc_tr128_q keeps the tiles, the MMAs and the K order (outputs are bit-identical: same hashes as the shipped kernel on gate+up and down) and changes the data path:
K256 stages (128 B of every weight row = one line, 192 B of every activation row copied raw, scales read from the raw rows, 16 B of weight scales per row),
one barrier per stage, a persistent grid with one continuous two-buffer ring across work items (the next item's first stage is loaded while the current one ends,
its metadata is fetched one item ahead), no zero-fill of the padding rows.

Bench (one layer, M=4096): gate+up 1.169 -> 0.709 ms, down 0.576 -> 0.393 ms (pair) / 0.640 -> 0.450 ms (atomic with its 38 MB clear).
Engine, RTX 5090, SQ-20, fast mode, 3 interleaved rounds: prefill 4K 20.5k -> 24.3k tok/s, 32K 18.7k -> 21.6k, 128K 12.9k -> 14.2k.
STRATA_INK_SM120_PFQ=0 runs the shipped kernel. Exact mode does not use this kernel.
```

### `9faad3a300bc` — inkling cuda: prompt checkpoints save into page-locked buffers, 20-40 ms less time to the first token

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_cuda_backend.cu` (+62/-8)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_moe_fp4_sm120.cu` (+8/-2)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_moe_fp4_sm120.cuh` (+3/-0)

- Related decisions:
  - No direct matrix decision; accepted as upstream baseline.

**Original commit message**

```
save_checkpoint (the prompt-end snapshot of the ShortConv state, the 40 SWA ring windows and the DSpark context, about 90 MB) runs between the last prefill chunk and
the first token. It copied the windows with ~85 cudaMemcpyAsync calls into std::vector memory (pageable: staged copies, a zero-fill of the vectors on every resize,
page faults), 24 ms in an nsys trace of a 4K prompt, 25-50 ms of the time to the first token at 8K..32K.

The checkpoint vectors use a page-locked allocator that default-initialises (no zero-fill), the target size is reserved once before the copies (they are asynchronous now,
the vectors must not move), and the memory is kept between requests. Falls back to ordinary memory when the page-locked allocation fails. The bytes are the same.

RTX 5090, SQ-20, 3 interleaved rounds, wall time to the first token minus the engine's prompt time: 8K 54 -> 9 ms, 32K 48 -> 25 ms (4K first request in a process: 165 -> 77 ms).
Also adds sm120::pfq_override() (a test hook for the teacher-forced KL harness: scores the K256 expert kernels against the shipped ones in one process).
```

### `2cbe802195d8` — perfstep 3: build the CUDA runner for sm_120a / sm_121a too: the FP4 -> half2 conversion becomes the hardware instruction (plain decode +10..13 % on a 5090)

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/CMakeLists.txt` (+13/-1)

- Related decisions:
  - No direct matrix decision; accepted as upstream baseline.

**Original commit message**

```
The runner translation unit (ink_cuda_backend.cu) was built for plain sm_120 / compute_120 only. There, __nv_cvt_fp4x2_to_halfraw2 (E2M1 -> half2) is not the hardware
cvt.rn.f16x2.e2m1x2 (an arch-specific instruction of sm_120a) but an expansion of about a hundred integer instructions (SASS: LOP3 / IMAD / PRMT / SEL). The M=1
expert kernels (k_ink_moe_gu_m1_l1, k_ink_moe_dn_m1_l1, the old path's decode) were therefore instruction-bound, not memory-bound: with the weights already in L2 they
took the same time as with them in DRAM.

Standalone bench of the real kernels (RTX 5090, 8 experts, cold = from DRAM): gate+up 25.7 -> 17.9 us, down 15.8 -> 9.5 us; in L2: 22.4 -> 8.1 and 14.1 -> 4.7 us.
CMake: the runner target keeps its architecture list and gets sm_120a-real / sm_121a-real in addition (CUDA 12.8+, one a-variant per Blackwell entry); the plain sm_120 SASS
and the compute_120 PTX stay for every other card. The driver picks the a-variant on an exact match (checked: a fatbin with both runs the fast code on the 5090).

End to end on the 5090 (plain decode, no drafter, 4 rounds, tokens identical between old and new build in all 12 runs of fast, exact and off, at 30, 3000 and 9000 tokens):
fast 179.0 -> 203.2 tok/s, exact 139.4 -> 153.5, off (old path) 139.4 -> 153.6.
```

**Original commit message**

```
Offered, in this order in setup, the launcher, `strata launch` and the app's model table: SQ-20, SQ-15, SQ-10, GSQ-RCO-IQ3_S, UD-Q4_K_XL.

Files and pins (strata_control/inkling_family.py, inkling_fit.py):
- SQ-20 is the re-adjusted 16 KiB-layout file Inkling-2-Mini-SQv2-20.strata (19,374,768,128 bytes, sha256 429671f8...); the Vision shard is
  Inkling-2-Mini-Vision-v2.strata (636,551,168 bytes, 34a17650...), used with every pack. The shown name and the model id stay SQ-20 /
  inkling-2-mini-sq-20.
- Every .strata download (packs, Vision, tokenizer files) is pinned to commit 5623ceb47d7a of strata-engine/Inkling-2-Mini-Strata (STRATA_PACK_COMMIT),
  which holds both v2 files and still the 0.2.22 files.
- An install that has the old SQ-20 or the old Vision file keeps running it (model_file_in, vision_in, LEGACY_FILES), is marked "update available" in the
  model table, the Terminal list and the app, and gets the new file only when the person installs again. No old file is ever deleted.
- Unsloth: UD-Q4_K_XL is the refreshed file (17,629,760,736 bytes, e6427665...) at commit 8078d149286e; the other nine quants are no longer offered,
  but an install that has one keeps working and the lists show it as installed, "no longer offered" (INKLING_FAMILIES legacy_models, ModelRow.offered).
- GSQ-RCO IQ3_S (ISTA-DASLab, Apache-2.0): Inkling-2-Mini-GSQ-RCO-IQ3_S.gguf, 11,425,259,648 bytes, sha256 8a351537..., pinned to commit
  357051619a88. Planner figures (routed experts 9.52 GB, eight expert types) are read from the file's GGUF header; credit "GSQ + RCO by IST Austria
  DASLab, Apache-2.0".

Drafter: DFlash 2 only, on every device. setup and update fetch dflash2.safetensors beside every Inkling model that Strata downloaded (packs and GGUFs);
no DSpark GGUF and no mtp.safetensors is fetched, passed (--dspark) or offered any more. Without the file the model starts with --draft off and
the launcher does not probe. The status word is dflash2. An old config that names the DSpark file still starts.

Also: the Terminal and `strata launch` lists carry the credit line, the no-longer-offered mark and the update mark; models.py reads SQv2 and GSQ file names;
the release grid pins follow the new tables; docs (README and translations, MODELS.md, DOCKER.md) and the DFlash 2 notice credit tjm8874.

Tests: terminal/tests/terminal/test_inkling_lineup.py (pins, order, hidden-but-installed, update offer, no DSpark fetch, DFlash 2 for every model);
the older Inkling tests follow the new tables.
```

### `45785bda0cdf` — perfstep 4: INT8-screened exact top-128 of the head for one row (STRATA_INK_HEAD_SCREEN, on in the 5090 fast set, off in exact mode)

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_cuda_backend.cu` (+48/-2)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_head_screen.cuh` (+199/-0)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_runner.cuh` (+27/-0)

- Related decisions:
  - No direct matrix decision; accepted as upstream baseline.

**Original commit message**

```
Plain decode reads the 883 MiB BF16 unembed (0.55 ms) and then runs a three-kernel top-k (0.08 ms) every step. The new path reads the 460 MB INT8 screening index of
the unembed instead (the exact Cauchy-Schwarz index the drafting argmax already uses) and returns the same 128 keys:
  1. INT8 dot of every row against the FP32 hidden state, bounds a_i -/+ e_i*|h| (e_i the stored residual norm), a histogram of the lower bounds on the way;
  2. T = lower edge of the highest histogram bin where 128 rows are reached (at least 128 rows have lb >= T, so a row with ub < T cannot be in the top 128);
  3. the rows with ub >= T (about 580 per step) get their exact logit with the arithmetic of k_ink_gemv_multi_bf16<1,false> (lane split, FMA order, 16-lane butterfly)
     and the softcap of k_ink_softcap: the same floats; keys by ink_topk_key; the top-k's own bitonic sort;
  4. more than 2048 candidates, no bin reaching 128 (NaN) or a failed guard: the host runs the full path for that step.
The finite flag comes from the screening dots at every 67th row (the full path reads the logits there).

STRATA_INK_HEAD_SCREEN_CHECK=1 runs both paths and compares every key and the flag: 0 differ in 200+ steps. STRATA_INK_HEAD_SCREEN=0/1 overrides the default (unset: on when the
fast set of the card has the FP8 trunk cache, i.e. the 5090 row; the INT8 index is then built even with --draft off, +0.46 GB VRAM).
5090, plain decode, 3 rounds, tokens identical with and without in 9/9 runs (30 / 3000 / 9000 tokens): 203.1 -> 212.3, 184.1 -> 189.4, 173.9 -> 178.9 tok/s (+4.5 / +2.9 / +2.9 %).
Exact mode (default off) is unchanged: exact == off 9/9. With drafting on nothing changes (the drafted steps use the argmax screen already).
```

### `d9d285068fe0` — feat(inkling2-cuda): DFlash 2 block-diffusion drafter for Inkling 2 Mini (sidecar dflash2.safetensors), long-context window cost, 2-block chain

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_cuda_backend.cu` (+14/-4)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_dflash2.cuh` (+559/-0)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_dflash2_kernels.cuh` (+718/-0)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_dflash2_path.cuh` (+61/-0)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_dspark.cuh` (+137/-0)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_rtx5090_bspec.cuh` (+10/-0)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_runner.cuh` (+6/-3)

- Related decisions:
  - No direct matrix decision; accepted as upstream baseline.

**Original commit message**

```
Adds Inkling 2's DFlash 2 drafter (incoai/Inkling-2-Mini-DFlash2, Apache-2.0) behind the existing drafter entry points. It is read from a sidecar safetensors file next to the model
(dflash2.safetensors, or STRATA_INK_DFLASH2=<file or dir>), like mtp.safetensors. kDflash2Default (ink_dflash2_path.cuh) makes it the default drafter; STRATA_INK_DRAFTER=dspark selects the
dormant DSpark code.

The drafter's design follows tjm8874's DFlash 2 implementation (MIT): 5 non-causal sliding-window layers wrapped in 2-tap group-16 dynamic convolutions, a rank-256 bilinear candidate
selector over the exact top-16 of the target's head, the full-vocabulary tail for the confidence. The top-16 and selector kernels are adapted from it (notice: notices/DFLASH2-TJM8874-MIT.txt);
the rest is written for Strata's runner: a safetensors loader, Q8 (or, on 16 GB cards, 4-bit grouped) block weights with a dp4a skinny GEMV, bf16 cuBLAS for the K/V and convolution matrices,
per-slot sliding-window context rings (41 MiB each), a batched split-K block attention, one int8 tensor-core pass over the target's Q8 unembed for the head, selector codebooks in mapped host
memory on 16 GB cards.

Hooks: taps ingest, batched draft, the tree-verify chain, the RTX 5090 batched path (ink_rtx5090_bspec.cuh) and the 5060 Ti multi-user chain; reserves (K/V sizing, the planner's pad) follow the
drafter's memory. Long context: on small cards a verify row costs about 2.2e-5 plain steps per context token, so from about 25K tokens the engine decodes plain steps and keeps no PLD rows;
a 2-block chain with a masked gap and a confidence exponent; STRATA_INK_DFLASH2_PROF prints the GPU time of the drafter.

Measured (RTX 5090 and RTX 5060 Ti 16 GB, SQ-20 v2): phaseA-tests/rel0223/dflash2/RESULTS.md.

Credit: tjm8874 for the DFlash 2 drafter design and the two kernels adapted from it; weights incoai/Inkling-2-Mini-DFlash2 (Apache-2.0).
```

### `7a0d50b15fd9` — feat(inkling2-cuda): DFlash 2 block weights in bf16, Q8, 4-bit groups or SF4 (STRATA_INK_DFLASH2_WQ), reserves follow the format

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_cuda_backend.cu` (+2/-2)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_dflash2.cuh` (+51/-18)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_dflash2_kernels.cuh` (+117/-0)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_dflash2_path.cuh` (+37/-0)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_dspark.cuh` (+1/-1)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_runner.cuh` (+1/-1)

- Related decisions:
  - No direct matrix decision; accepted as upstream baseline.

**Original commit message**

```
The drafter's block weights (attention q / o, feed-forward gate / up / down; 332 M parameters) can now be held in four formats per group, chosen by STRATA_INK_DFLASH2_WQ:
bf16 (the reference, cuBLAS), q8 (Q8 row-scaled, as DSpark is stored in SQ-20), q4 (groups of 32, fp16 scale), sf4 (Strata's FMT_SF4 layout: E2M1 x 2 nibbles, a UE4M3 scale
per 16 values, one fp32 tensor scale; decoded through a shared-memory table to int8 for dp4a), mix (attention Q8, feed-forward SF4) and mixq4 (attention Q8, feed-forward 4-bit groups).
Unset: 4-bit groups on cards below 24 GB, Q8 above (what shipped before). Everything else stays bf16 / f32: fc, the K / V and convolution projections, the selector and its
codebooks, the norms; the target's embedding and unembed are the target's own. The weights are quantized from the bf16 sidecar when the engine starts.

The start-up reserve, the K/V sizing reserve and the loader's pad now follow the chosen format (ink_dflash2_path.cuh: df2_total_mib).

Credit: tjm8874 for the DFlash 2 drafter design and the two kernels adapted from it (MIT, notices/DFLASH2-TJM8874-MIT.txt); weights incoai/Inkling-2-Mini-DFlash2 (Apache-2.0).
```

### `a35a0c15054f` — feat(inkling2-cuda): DFlash 2 is the only Inkling drafter on CUDA; pre-quantized sidecar (strata-dflash2-q1); format by card; no drafter without the sidecar

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_cuda_backend.cu` (+2/-1)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_dflash2.cuh` (+125/-5)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_dflash2_kernels.cuh` (+5/-7)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_dflash2_path.cuh` (+60/-17)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_dspark.cuh` (+22/-10)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_runner.cuh` (+2/-2)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/ink_serve.h` (+2/-2)

- Related decisions:
  - No direct matrix decision; accepted as upstream baseline.

**Original commit message**

```
DFlash 2 is the drafter for every slot count (the 16-slot cap is gone; up to 64 slots on cards of 24 GiB and more, 16 below) and for every build whose sidecar
(dflash2.safetensors beside the model, or STRATA_INK_DFLASH2) is found, GGUF builds included: the drafter no longer needs DSpark weights in the model file
(has_dspark means "a drafter is there"). Without the sidecar, or when it cannot start, the run has no drafter: it does not fall back to DSpark. The DSpark code is dormant;
STRATA_INK_DRAFTER=dspark selects it, and only then are its weights loaded onto the card.

Sidecar: STRATA_INK_DFLASH2_DUMP=<file> writes the block weights as they sit on the card (the engine's own quantization) as a safetensors file, format "strata-dflash2-q1":
NAME.q (I8 or U8 codes), NAME.s (F32 per row / F16 per 32 / UE4M3 per 16), NAME.o (F32 tensor scale, SF4), the other tensors under the checkpoint's names (norms as F32), metadata
{"format": "strata-dflash2-q1", "variant": ...}. The loader takes the format from the file. The mix (Q8 attention, SF4 feed-forward) is 551,600,700 bytes against 989,975,616 for
the original; Q8 656 MB, 4-bit groups and SF4 510 MB. Each sidecar loaded back gives the same tokens as the same format quantized at load (exact mode, 3 fixtures).
Format when the file is the original checkpoint: BF16 on cards of 24 GiB and more, the mix below (measured, phaseA-tests/rel0223/dflash2/RESULTS.md). The load time is printed.

The credit in the sources names tjm8874 only; the MIT notice for the two kernels adapted from the DFlash 2 implementation is notices/DFLASH2-TJM8874-MIT.txt.
```

### `0cff0e64b154` — fix(inkling2-cuda): DFlash 2 at 32 and 64 slots, and a drafter that cannot start gives its memory back

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_dflash2.cuh` (+24/-6)

- Related decisions:
  - No direct matrix decision; accepted as upstream baseline.

**Original commit message**

```
32 slots: the attention partials were sized for B x split <= 24 (split is 1 above 24 slots, so B x split = B): 32 slots wrote past them (illegal memory access, RTX 5090, fast mode,
--parallel 32). 64 slots: the context rings of all slots took every free byte of a 32 GB card and the engine died in an allocation of the multi-slot path (out of memory, 6 MiB free);
the rings of slots 8 and up are now taken only while 1 GiB stays free, a slot without a ring decodes plainly. A drafter that failed to start (no room for the rings) left its weights
and scratch on the card (RTX 5060 Ti, bf16 forced: 21 MiB free, the engine died at the first request); free_all() now frees every buffer it owns, so the run goes on without a drafter.

Credit: tjm8874 for the DFlash 2 drafter design; weights incoai/Inkling-2-Mini-DFlash2 (Apache-2.0).
```

  - `mcp-upstream-features`: upstream — Берём stale-ok hardware refresh, custom models, Inkling legacy/update metadata; наша own-size строка сохраняется.

### `34cf13848e6c` — fix(file tier): keep the GGUF stage pool when the start-up unbuffered reads end

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `engine/include/strata/core/expert_source.hpp` (+6/-2)
  - `engine/src/core/expert_source.cpp` (+26/-13)
  - `engine/tests/core/expert_layout_test.cpp` (+58/-0)

- Related decisions:
  - No direct matrix decision; accepted as upstream baseline.

**Original commit message**

```
Since 0.1.67 begin_startup_unbuffered also takes the GGUF shards read in
place (it said "experts.bin only" before). Its end step then ran for GGUF
packs too and cleared the stage pool and set stage_blob_ to 0. The GGUF
tier needs that pool for the rest of the run: blob() assembles every expert
the RAM copy lacks into a stage buffer. With stage_blob_ == 0 the next
claim_stage allocated a zero-byte buffer and fill_stage copied a whole
blob (about 1.5 MB) into it: a heap overrun, seen on Windows CUDA as a
memcpy access violation, 0xC0000374 or a fault in a new thread's TLS
allocation, right after the first window starts (#7131, #7155, #7206,
and the copy_from_files crash of #7123).

- end_startup_unbuffered: the stage-pool step is release_startup_stage(),
  which only runs for experts.bin (and now also frees the stage events
  like close() does).
- begin_startup_unbuffered: the failure path no longer zeroes stage_blob_
  for the GGUF in place.
- claim_stage refuses to make a buffer when no buffer size is known.
- expert_layout_test: a cross-platform check (release_startup_stage, then
  blob() on the GGUF in place) and a Windows check of the begin/end steps.
  Before the fix the first one is an AddressSanitizer SEGV in
  copy_from_files; after it, clean.

(cherry picked from commit d40f3fd8b8d8ac01fadd7aa0635aed6674f747bc)
```

### `99e881584a1f` — inkling2: opt-in backend hooks from the owner's HIP branches (sampled spec, async prompt checkpoint, decoder-aware prefill cap, BCO prewarm)

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `engine/src/inkling2/ink_backend.h` (+37/-0)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/ink_serve.h` (+65/-5)

- Related decisions:
  - No direct matrix decision; accepted as upstream baseline.

**Original commit message**

```
Cherry-pick of 8211481a9 (owner/gpu-r9700; f3255adc7 on owner/gpu-strixhalo is the same tree, so the hooks land once).
All four hooks default to "not offered" (false / 0), so the serve loop is the shipped one on CUDA, Metal, SYCL, CPU and every HIP card that does not opt in.

Adapted to the 0.2.23 base:
- The owner's batched multi-slot speculative hook (supports_batched_spec / dspark_spec_step_batched, its serve-loop block 2b and its --bench branch) is NOT taken.
  The sm_120 base already has that feature as supports_batched_dspark / dspark_spec_step_batched_greedy with the same serve-loop block 2b and --bench branch;
  one hook per feature. The HIP R9700 and Strix backends implement the existing one in the next commits.
- use_dspark keeps the base's form (backend.dspark_ready()).
- The async-checkpoint pause rule is applied to both prefill branches (the C64 prefill-first one and the per-slot one).
```

### `23aaa0fdc13b` — inkling2(hip): add opt-in Radeon AI PRO R9700 32GB (gfx1201) exact and fast paths

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `engine/src/inkling2/hip/CMakeLists.txt` (+5/-0)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/hip/ink_hip_backend.hip.cpp` (+1763/-252)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/hip/ink_hip_r9700_attn.hip.h` (+765/-0)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/hip/ink_hip_r9700_cfg.hpp` (+167/-0)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/hip/ink_hip_r9700_fp8.hip.h` (+2631/-0)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/hip/ink_hip_r9700_spec.hip.h` (+627/-0)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/hip/ink_hip_r9700_state.hpp` (+618/-0)

- Related decisions:
  - No direct matrix decision; accepted as upstream baseline.

**Original commit message**

```
Adds strictly opt-in R9700 32GB acceleration gated behind STRATA_INK_R9700=1 and a per-ordinal 0..15 hardware check (gfx1201, discrete, >=28 WGPs/CUs, >=28 GiB, R9700 name; rejects 16 GB RX 9070/XT and all other cards with a 1-line warning). All 37 R9700 device kernels are guarded with __gfx1201__ for zero fatbin bloat on other targets, with zero default-path drift when OFF. Includes EXACT mode (STRATA_INK_R9700_EXACT=1, 100% greedy bit-identical to OFF, KLD=0.000000) and FAST mode (native gfx1201 FP8 WMMA trunk with per-64 outlier quarantine, zero-spill persistent FP8/FP16 WMMA MoE, fused in-tile d_rel=16 FlashAttention, fixed swa_ring=1024, WinPath16 + PLD + on-GPU stochastic tree verify, and C64 top-128 candidate extraction).

(cherry picked from commit 8bfe645e1e8db6cdd460145e6bfb095d24304e8e)
```

### `e4fdcef21e64` — inkling2(hip): add opt-in AMD Strix Halo (gfx1151) UMA/WDDM/SF4/WMMA optimizations, merged with the R9700 path

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `engine/src/inkling2/hip/CMakeLists.txt` (+5/-0)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/hip/ink_hip_backend.hip.cpp` (+2382/-66)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/hip/ink_hip_strixhalo_cfg.hpp` (+205/-0)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/hip/ink_hip_strixhalo_decode.hip.h` (+2688/-0)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/hip/ink_hip_strixhalo_megakernel.hip.h` (+710/-0)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/hip/ink_hip_strixhalo_prefill.hip.h` (+1567/-0)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/hip/ink_hip_strixhalo_uma.hip.h` (+771/-0)

- Related decisions:
  - No direct matrix decision; accepted as upstream baseline.

**Original commit message**

```
Cherry-pick of 8e2379127 (owner/gpu-strixhalo) on top of the R9700 commit (8bfe645e1). Both opt-ins live in ink_hip_backend.hip.cpp; they are for different cards
(R9700 gfx1201 discrete, Strix Halo gfx1151 UMA) and never run in the same process, so every shared spot dispatches on r9700_ first, then halo_, then the shipped code.

Merge decisions:
- Hooks: one batched multi-slot speculative hook (supports_batched_dspark / dspark_spec_step_batched_greedy, the base's); both cards implement it through
  dspark_spec_step_batched_r9700 and dspark_spec_step_batched_halo. One dspark_spec_step_sampled override dispatches to the R9700 body or the Strix one.
- init: the Strix block is skipped when the R9700 gate took the card.
- forward_batch_chunk takes both optional outputs (candidate rows for the R9700, the argmax rows for the Strix Halo).
- Attention dispatch: R9700 chunk attention, then Strix fused attention, then the shipped chain; the Strix MoE I8/IU4 branches sit under the shipped gen check behind the R9700 FP8 branch.
- invalidate_graph() on a grown logits buffer runs only when one of the two opt-ins is active (the R9700 branch had made it unconditional).
```

**Original commit message**

```
- Merge fixes: the closing brace of the R9700 post-init block, the Strix argmax call sites pass the new fifth parameter (the R9700's candidate rows are the fourth).
- Strix SF4+outlier-16 dense trunk (qkv/r/gate and wo) and the Coda MX8 -> SF4 transcode skip layers 5, 11, 17 and 23: the drafter taps the hidden state after
  those layers, and 4-bit weights there cut its acceptance (TRUNK4: 45% default, 32% with 4-bit everywhere, 46% with these layers kept). They keep their shipped
  format. STRATA_INK_STRIXHALO_SF4_TAPS=1 puts them in 4-bit as the owner's first version did (measurement only). Layers that are skipped do not clear sf4_trunk_ready.
```

### `60aedcda9956` — inkling2 HIP: DFlash 2 drafter port for RDNA2 / RDNA3 (wave32)

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221

  - `engine/src/inkling2/hip/ink_hip_backend.hip.cpp` (+133/-2)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/hip/ink_hip_df2.hip.h` (+1342/-0)

- Related decisions:
  - No direct matrix decision; accepted as upstream baseline.

**Original commit message**

```
New ink_hip_df2.hip.h: the CUDA DFlash 2 drafter (sidecar dflash2.safetensors) in HIP kernels: tap gather, fc + per-layer K/V ingest into per-slot
2112-cell rings, 5 block layers with dynamic 2-tap convolutions, Q8 / 4-bit skinny GEMV on sdot4/sudot4, split-K sliding-window attention, a row-by-row
head over the trunk's own unembed format (fp32 logits of the 7 draft rows, chunked exact top-16 + tail), and the bilinear selector.
The backend hooks are one-liners: ingest inside the existing tap / graph path, draft in dspark_draft, rollback / reset of the ring bookkeeping.
Default block weights are Q8 on every card; codebooks go to mapped host memory below 24 GB.
```

### `369a6eac32be` — sm120: compact keeps 128 MiB free (K/V that does not fit goes to mapped host memory), prints its sizing

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_runner.cuh` (+19/-0)

- Related decisions:
  - No direct matrix decision; accepted as upstream baseline.

**Original commit message**

```
An RTX 5080 (driver 595, CUDA 13.0 build) ended the compact load with 104 MiB free after the K/V floor (7680 device tokens) and its engine died at the first top-k
(ink_cuda_backend.cu:995 "invalid argument", "Tree CUDA Graph capture unavailable (err=2)"), 4 times in one FASTQ run; an RTX 5070 Ti ended with 149 MiB free and ran.
CUDA_MODULE_LOADING=EAGER does not help (tested: eager costs about 60 MiB more at start and the engine still died).
The compact sizing now keeps 128 MiB free after the workspaces, the SWA ring and the K/V: device K/V above what fits goes to the Auto Host KV Offload (mapped host memory).
On the 5080 that is 2816 device tokens instead of 7680 and 182 MiB free after load. Cards with room keep all of it (the 5060 Ti default has not been re-measured with this change: it ended with about 140 MiB free before, so it may now also move a little K/V to the host).
One stderr line "[Ink5060Ti] compact sizing: ..." gives the numbers; "compact keeps 128 MiB free" says when the cut applied.
```

### `82623cd60c04` — fix(control): `strata launch` no longer freezes on a key press while the hardware is read again (#7183)

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `terminal/setup.py` (+56/-12)
  - `terminal/strata_cli/launch/catalog.py` (+3/-1)
  - `terminal/tests/terminal/test_hardware_stall_7183.py` (+252/-0)
  - `terminal/tools/strata_mcp.py` (+65/-3)

- Related decisions:
  - `setup-kat-huihui`: ours — Upstream добавляет Ornith/custom builds, но не KAT/Huihui pins и per-model own_file/own_hf.
  - `setup-upstream-features`: upstream — Берём Ornith, custom-label, hardware refresh, DFlash2 и setup error hint; KAT/Huihui остаются отдельной ours-строкой.
  - `mcp-own-size`: ours — Upstream не заменяет наш per-model own_size lookup.
  - `mcp-upstream-features`: upstream — Берём stale-ok hardware refresh, custom models, Inkling legacy/update metadata; наша own-size строка сохраняется.

**Original commit message**

```
On Windows the shared hardware reader kept its answer for 600 s and then read the PC on the caller's thread: seven child processes, four of them a
fresh PowerShell start (the CPU name and three Win32_VideoController queries). Reporter's numbers: 12 s per read on a Ryzen 7 5700X + Radeon AI PRO
R9700. The Terminal's launch screens ask for the hardware on a key press, so the first value-changing key after ten idle minutes froze it.

- Strata.hardware(stale_ok=True): an answer older than 600 s is returned at once and one background thread reads the PC again for the next call
  (never two at once; a read that fails keeps the old answer). The first read is still waited for; fresh=True still reads now. The Terminal's
  Catalog asks with stale_ok, the app's own readers keep their rule.
- Windows: the display adapters are read ONCE per hardware read (setup.win_video_scope / win_video_rows: name, PNP id, RAM, driver, resolution) and
  feed amd_gpus, windows_desktop_adapters and windows_video_controllers. Four PowerShell starts become one. Outside a read nothing is remembered.
- Windows: the CPU name comes from the registry (HKLM\HARDWARE\DESCRIPTION\System\CentralProcessor\0), PowerShell only when it cannot be read.

Not explained and not touched: the reporter's other seven seconds (not in any wrapped call); a profile on a Windows PC with the slow start-up is the
way to settle it. Not run on Windows here: the child process and the registry are fakes in the tests.

Tests: terminal/tests/terminal/test_hardware_stall_7183.py.
```

- Related decisions:

**Original commit message**

```
A tool call that failed (a page that did not open) kept its red "failed" row after the same call went through later in the turn: the page said "failed"
about a step that had worked.

- Host (agent/events.mjs): when a call works, the earlier FAILED cards of the same tool with the same arguments in this turn get `superseded: true`.
  Only an identical call counts (the argument keys are compared in a fixed order). The same goal by another tool or other arguments cannot be told from
  a new step without guessing, so that row stays. The card keeps state "error": it did fail.
- Page: a superseded failed card is not drawn (lib/tool-cards.ts shownToolCard, used by the chat).
- New setting Settings > General > "Hide failed steps": no failed card is drawn at all (a question waiting for the person and a refused call always
  are). The default is ONE constant, features.ts HIDE_FAILED_TOOLS_BY_DEFAULT = false (show, as before); the owner's decision flips that line. The
  person's own choice is saved only once made, so changing the default reaches everyone who never touched it. Strings in all 10 locales.

Tests: app/host/tests/failed-tool-rows.test.mjs.
```

### `c282ce6c40a2` — fix(serve): an engine crash the system reports is no longer blamed on RAM (#7245)

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `terminal/serve/server.py` (+19/-4)
  - `terminal/serve/test_server.py` (+66/-1)

- Related decisions:
  - `qwen36-200k-launch`: ours — Upstream не заменяет fork launch profiles и static checker.

**Original commit message**

```
The note the server writes when the engine died (death_note) ended with "The usual cause is running out of RAM ... Windows slows down instead" for
every death nothing else explained. The 0.1.69 Windows host-copy overrun ended the engine with 0xC0000005 (an access violation) and the reporter was
told to close programs.

RAM is now named only where something points at it: the engine's own log (bad_alloc, out of memory), the kernel's kill (signal 9), or on Linux and
macOS an exit nobody can read. A crash the system reports (an NTSTATUS crash code such as an access violation, a stack overrun, an illegal
instruction; SIGSEGV, SIGILL, SIGBUS, SIGFPE) says "the engine crashed ... a fault in the engine itself, not a lack of memory ... report it with the
engine log" and, on Windows, names the Application error entry of Event Viewer. On Windows with no exit status the note says the cause is not known and
is not known to be a memory problem. A Windows crash code no longer takes the "exited (code N), its last log line" sentence either.

The engine fix for the crash itself is 34cf13848. Tests: serve/test_server.py (SilentEngine); the old case that expected the RAM text for SIGSEGV now
expects the crash text.
```

### `527612af6244` — feat(serve): opt-in thinking level at the end of the prompt for Inkling-2-Mini, Control half (#7241)

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `docs/DETAILS.md` (+1/-1)
  - `terminal/serve/inkling_dialect.py` (+26/-0)
  - `terminal/serve/server.py` (+19/-2)
  - `terminal/serve/test_inkling_effort_tail.py` (+167/-0)

- Related decisions:
  - `qwen36-200k-launch`: ours — Upstream не заменяет fork launch profiles и static checker.

**Original commit message**

```
The Inkling template writes `Thinking effort level: X` before the first non-system message, so a request at another level starts differently and the
engine reads the whole conversation again (reporter, RTX 3090: +10 s at 20K tokens, +29 s at 50K, +71 s at 100K).

With the config's "effort_position": "end" (until now ignored for Inkling) the history is rendered at the default level (STRATA_INKLING_EFFORT, low)
and a request at another level adds the template's own system turn `<|message_system|><|content_text|>Thinking effort level: X<|end_message|>` at the
end, right before the answer; level 0 still gets the text opener, thinking levels nothing. A request at the default level, a prefilled reply and every
config without the option render exactly as before. The strata_prefix head follows the same rule.

effort_end_args knows Inkling: it passes --tail-role-token with the id of <|message_system|> only when the engine binary has that flag. No Inkling
engine has it yet, so today the option is refused at start with a plain sentence and the level stays at the top. The engine half (a checkpoint in
front of the trailing system turn, as the main engine's #458) is NOT done; the level at the end also blends with the level at the top (reporter:
header 0.9 + tail 0.2 gave 485 thinking tokens between 253 and 712), so it stays opt-in until measured.

Tests: serve/test_inkling_effort_tail.py (real template: prefix kept across a level change, openers, prefill, config switch, nothing changes
without the option). docs/DETAILS.md.
```

- Related decisions:
  - `readme-fork-notes`: ours — Upstream не заменяет fork-заметки, research-ссылки и диаграммы измерений.
  - `readme-upstream-catalog`: upstream — Добавляем Ornith/Inkling 0.2.23 и release 0.2.23, не удаляя fork-блоки.
  - `setup-kat-huihui`: ours — Upstream добавляет Ornith/custom builds, но не KAT/Huihui pins и per-model own_file/own_hf.

  - `qwen36-200k-launch`: ours — Upstream не заменяет fork launch profiles и static checker.

**Original commit message**

```
Ornith-1.5-35B-A3B has the architecture, tensor layout, tokenizer and MTP
block of Qwen3.6-35B-A3B (qwen35moe), so it runs on the same engine path.
It is now a family of its own in the shared table: the four plain
quantizations the publisher's GGUF repository has (Q4_K_M, Q5_K_M, Q6_K,
Q8_0), pinned to one revision with sizes and SHA-256 values (checked against
Hugging Face), the picture reader, the MIT licence line, KV and fit figures
(q36's KV and state, a base figure with the dense part swapped; the staging
figures are q36's and marked as not measured for Ornith).

- `strata models import PATH --plan` reads an Ornith-named file as family
  ornith with its own size; any other name stays qwen36.
- Model ids "ornith-1.5-..." are Strata ids in `strata serve` and the app's
  gateway; the picture step leaves Ornith to setup like Qwen3.6; the launch
  display name is the family's.
- App: Ornith is its own display group after Qwen3.6 (its title contains
  "35B-A3B", which put it under Qwen3.6), with no KV choice like Qwen3.6, and
  the short name "Ornith Q4_K_M".
- Docs: a section in docs/MODELS.md and the README.
- Tests: terminal/tests/terminal/test_ornith_types.py with four fixtures (the
  tensor type tables read from the GGUF headers at the pinned revision),
  test_ornith_catalog_7134.py (model list, run config, pictures, model ids),
  app/host/tests/ornith-family.test.mjs.

Not measured by us: a load and answer of an Ornith file on our own box.

Patch and tests by eSPiYa (PR #7140)
```

### `6a121e1ea043` — sm120 table: a DGX Spark (GB10, cc 12.1) row, matched by name + SM count + memory + cc minor; a placeholder until measured

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `engine/include/strata/core/ink_sm120_table.hpp` (+20/-3)
  - `engine/tests/core/ink_sm120_table_test.cpp` (+36/-2)

- Related decisions:
  - No direct matrix decision; accepted as upstream baseline.

**Original commit message**

```
The table knew cc 12.0 only and refused any switch on a GB10 ("not an sm_120 card"). Now a row carries the cc minor it is for (default 0), the
matcher takes cc 12.0 and 12.1 and a row never matches the other minor, so the 5090 row cannot be taken by a GB10 and the GB10 row cannot be taken
by an RTX card. The new row "gb10" (name GB10, 44..52 SMs, 100..128 GiB) carries the 5090's "big" set (FP8 trunk cache, NVFP4 MoE, PF_ATTN,
DSPARK_OPT, C64 bspec; exact = DSPARK_OPT) and the 5090's numbers. It is not measured and not default: with no switch the old path runs; the fast
set is reached with STRATA_INK_SM120=1 STRATA_INK_SM120_ROW=gb10 until the lane's measurements flip measured / default_on.
Table test: the Spark cases (placeholder note, ROW=gb10 fast and exact, a part off, =0 silent, no RTX row on cc 12.1, an unknown cc 12.1 card).
```

### `31c24ad0fa28` — inkling2(hip): R9700 EXACT keeps off's tokens; the single-pass prompt checkpoint is off in EXACT

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `engine/src/inkling2/hip/ink_hip_r9700_cfg.hpp` (+3/-1)

- Related decisions:
  - No direct matrix decision; accepted as upstream baseline.

**Original commit message**

```
STRATA_INK_R9700=1 STRATA_INK_R9700_EXACT=1 differed from off on 3 of 4 prompts (prose, code, count), with or without a drafter. Cause: ASYNC_CKPT (on in both modes) feeds the whole prompt in one
pass, so the last prompt token is computed inside the batch instead of alone after the prompt checkpoint, and on HIP a row inside a batch is not bit-identical to the same row alone.
Measured on p620 (R9700, SQ-20 v2, greedy 256 tokens, 2 rounds, tokens against the base binary): EXACT 0/2 identical on prose, code and count; EXACT with ASYNC_CKPT=0 2/2 on all four prompts, also with
DFlash 2 drafting. SPEC=0 and CAND=0 change nothing, so the drafting policy is not involved. ASYNC_CKPT now defaults to !exact; STRATA_INK_R9700_ASYNC_CKPT=1 still forces it.
```

### `c8a525ab1706` — sm120: COMPACT_HYBRID for 8 and 12 GB cards, probe-derived CPU/PCIe split, compact refuses 2-15 slots, default_on per row

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `engine/include/strata/core/ink_sm120_table.hpp` (+9/-1)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_hybrid.cuh` (+8/-2)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_runner.cuh` (+16/-2)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_sm120_cfg.cuh` (+4/-0)

- Related decisions:
  - No direct matrix decision; accepted as upstream baseline.

**Original commit message**

```
- New part COMPACT_HYBRID (STRATA_INK_SM120_COMPACT_HYBRID): when COMPACT cannot hold every expert (8 / 12 GB cards) the loader no longer refuses; it builds the
  compact trunk (Q8_ROW attention and head, mapped host embedding, about 2.3 GiB less than the BF16 trunk) and gives the freed VRAM to the adaptive expert tier, the rest
  of the experts stay in RAM. The KV stays FP16 unless COMPACT_KV is named (teacher-forced KL on the TAILSKIP screen: the FP8 KV is the costly part).
- The split of the batched (verify) cold experts between the CPU lane and PCIe comes from this box's own launch probe: 0.75 x t_cpu / (t_cpu + t_copy). Set only while
  COMPACT_HYBRID is on; every other path keeps the fixed 0.55 / 0.65. STRATA_INK_DEC_PCIE_SHARE still overrides it.
- COMPACT now refuses before it converts anything when the run asks for 2 to 15 slots (--parallel 4 on a 16 GB card died at start with "not enough free GPU memory");
  the old path runs. One slot and 16 or more slots are unchanged.
- Row field default_on: a measured row runs its fast set by default only when default_on is set (5090 and 5060 Ti 16 GB: true).
```

### `b60a5d2d0b70` — fix(inkling2-cuda): table rows for RTX 40 (sm_89) and RTX 30 / A-series (sm_86) with a per-family part mask

```
is dropped. COMPACT no longer requires cc >= 12.0 in the loader (the decision already refuses other families).
```

### `b3916be11d53` — inkling2 HIP: compact trunk for GSQ-RCO GGUF files (host embedding, Q8_ROW unembed) and a 256-token prefill chunk on cards of 13 GB or less

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `engine/src/inkling2/hip/ink_hip_backend.hip.cpp` (+16/-3)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/ink_main.cpp` (+6/-0)

- Related decisions:
  - No direct matrix decision; accepted as upstream baseline.

**Original commit message**

```
HIP-only builds let the shared GGUF loader compact the trunk of a file with the GSQ-RCO expert types (STRATA_INK_GGUF_COMPACT=0 / 1 forces it off / on): the unembed becomes
Q8_ROW (the kernels already read fmt 100), the 0.9 GB embedding table is the last tensor of Section 1 and the HIP backend keeps it in pinned mapped host RAM (a lookup reads one row
over PCIe), and vram accounting skips it. With embed_host_tail on a discrete card of 13 GB or less the backend reports a 256-token effective chunk and plans its scratch with it, so
more experts stay in the card (STRATA_INK_GGUF_CHUNK256=0 turns it off; --chunk N wins).
```

### `a7ab14947a92` — fix(app): keep port 47831, remember the fallback port, say when the system keeps the port back (#7244)

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `app/desktop/src-tauri/src/main.rs` (+22/-3)
  - `app/desktop/src-tauri/src/shell.rs` (+104/-2)
  - `app/host/server/shell-prefs.mjs` (+7/-3)
  - `app/host/src/components/ShellSettings.tsx` (+1/-1)
  - `app/host/src/lib/store.ts` (+3/-1)
  - `app/host/src/locales/de.json` (+2/-0)
  - `app/host/src/locales/en.json` (+2/-0)
  - `app/host/src/locales/es.json` (+2/-0)
  - `app/host/src/locales/fr.json` (+2/-0)
  - `app/host/src/locales/he.json` (+2/-0)
  - `app/host/src/locales/ja.json` (+2/-0)
  - `app/host/src/locales/ko.json` (+2/-0)
  - `app/host/src/locales/pt.json` (+2/-0)
  - `app/host/src/locales/ru.json` (+2/-0)
  - `app/host/src/locales/zh.json` (+2/-0)
  - `app/host/tests/port-reserved-7244.test.mjs` (+44/-0)

- Related decisions:
  - No direct matrix decision; accepted as upstream baseline.

**Original commit message**

```
On a Windows PC with WSL mirrored networking the usual port 47831 can sit in a block the system keeps back: a bind there fails like a port in use,
nothing lists it, and nothing answers on it. The app took a NEW random port at every start (the page's chats and settings belong to the port, #3402)
and said "another program uses the port".

- The app still asks for 47831 first (or the App port of Settings). The default is not changed: that would empty the saved chats of every install once.
- When the wanted port cannot be bound, the port taken is saved in <data folder>/shell-fallback-port.json (written by the shell only, never by the
  host, so it cannot meet the write of shell-prefs.json) and used again at the next start while it is free. A fallback saved for another wanted port
  (the person changed App port) is ignored.
- When a short connection attempt to the wanted port is refused (nobody listens) the notice is "reserved:<wanted>:<used>"; the host parses it, the
  page says the port is reserved by the system (often WSL mirrored networking, Hyper-V or Docker), which port it started on, that it will use that one
  again, and that Settings > App port chooses one. A port where a program answers keeps the old wording; a Terminal model server keeps its own. The
  attempt waits up to 4 s because a closed loopback port is refused only after the SYN retries on Windows (a first harness run with 800 ms gave
  TimedOut on the owner PC); it is paid only when the bind already failed.

Tests: src-tauri/src/shell.rs (cargo test of the pure functions on the owner PC through a harness crate holding shell.rs without the tauri tray module:
7 passed; main.rs itself is NOT compiled by that run, the Windows build compiles it), app/host/tests/port-reserved-7244.test.mjs. Strings in all 10 locales.
```

### `6b74e749ae8d` — packaging: every Linux CUDA package carries licenses/ (libgomp and libatomic notices)

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221

  - `terminal/tests/terminal/test_engine_deps_linux.py` (+2/-0)

**Original commit message**

```
collect_libs.py bundles libatomic.so.1 and libgomp.so.1 from the build machine
without a flag and writes their notices to <out>/licenses/. package_engine.py
without --tree packs top-level files only, so the notice reaches the package
only with --include 'licenses/*'. The Inkling stage of Dockerfile.src has it
since the previous commit; this adds it to the other packagers that run
collect_libs.py and package_engine.py:

- tools/docker/Dockerfile.src: the engine and Hy4 packages
- tools/packaging/build_cuda_linux.sh, build_cuda_linux_aarch64.sh
  (aarch64 packs what engine/spark/build_engine.sh collected)

A package without a licenses folder is unchanged (the glob matches nothing).

Tests: test_engine_deps_linux checks libgomp.so.1 is on the bundle list with
its notice; test_bundle_system_notices checks every package script that calls
collect_libs.py, and all three package_engine calls in Dockerfile.src, pass
--include 'licenses/*'.
```

### `f61646586538` — serve(inkling): with --parallel 2 a request that continues nothing no longer wipes a long chat (#7226)

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `terminal/serve/server.py` (+21/-4)
  - `terminal/serve/test_inkling_slot_routing.py` (+83/-0)
  - `terminal/serve/test_parallel.py` (+28/-0)

- Related decisions:
  - `qwen36-200k-launch`: ours — Upstream не заменяет fork launch profiles и static checker.

**Original commit message**

```
Two long conversations that alternate (an agent client's auto mode: the main chat and a large safety-check request) evicted each other from slot 0 on
every request while slot 1 stayed empty, so every request read its whole prompt again. Three causes, all in serve/server.py:

- _inkling_route sent a request elsewhere only if it was shorter than SIDE_REQUEST_MIN (2,048 tokens); a larger one that continued nothing took slot 0.
- With the DSpark drafter on, pick_slot preferred slot 0 for any request whose best held prefix was under 512 tokens (505e6cbbe), so even a short title
  request wiped the chat in slot 0.
- A later pass of a thinking budget (ckpt=0) overwrote slot_prompt, so the server forgot the prompt-end checkpoint the engine still keeps and sent the
  chat's next turn to another slot.

Now, while slot 0 holds a chat of SIDE_REQUEST_MIN tokens or more and another slot is free, a request that continues nothing takes an empty (else the least
recently used) slot whatever its size, the drafter's slot-0 preference gives way, and a ckpt=0 pass that extends the recorded prompt leaves it alone.
The chat that lives in another slot decodes without drafts (the drafter works in slot 0 only).

On by default; STRATA_INKLING_KEEP_CHATS=0 (or false / no / off) brings back the 0.2.22 routing exactly. One slot and other engines are unchanged.

Tests: serve/test_inkling_slot_routing.py (the reporter's alternating case with the drafter on and off, the switch at 0, a title request with the drafter on,
a wrap-up pass with the drafter on and off, one slot unchanged) and serve/test_parallel.py (pick_slot with the drafter: kept chat, switch values, no free slot).
```

### `7ae844c6025f` — serve(inkling): keep a chat in slot 0 only while it is alive; the solo path stamps slot 0 (#7226)

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221

  - `terminal/serve/server.py` (+12/-3)
  - `terminal/serve/test_parallel.py` (+4/-1)

- Related decisions:

**Original commit message**

```
The keep rule of the previous commit protects the chat in slot 0 from a request that continues nothing.  A lone client that starts a new conversation
after a pause would then be sent to slot 1 and lose the DSpark drafter (505e6cbbe: 152 vs 85 tok/s on the RTX 5090 it was measured on) while the
abandoned chat keeps slot 0.  Now the rule applies only while the chat in slot 0 was used within STRATA_INKLING_KEEP_IDLE_S seconds (default 600);
after that a request that continues nothing takes slot 0 with the drafter, as in 0.2.22.

The solo path (GEN, slot 0) never recorded that slot 0 was used, so the least-recently-used choice treated the chat that lives there as the oldest.
It now stamps slot_used[0] when it starts a request on an Inkling engine.

STRATA_INKLING_KEEP_CHATS=0 still brings back the 0.2.22 routing exactly.  Tests: serve/test_parallel.py (a chat that was not used for longer than the window is
not kept).
```

### `fd5fc4aa76ec` — engine: STRATA_PF_SYNC_DEBUG=1 names the fused expert launch that faults (#7115)

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `engine/src/prefill/moe_fused_iq.cu` (+34/-1)

- Related decisions:
  - `settings-fork-knobs`: ours — FDEC/DOWN_SUB8 и связанные fork-настройки отсутствуют в upstream delta.
  - `settings-upstream-register`: upstream — Берём upstream settings additions; итоговый register/docs перегенерировать после объединения.
  - `fused-sync-debug`: upstream — Берём default-off dbg_sync; он оборачивает launches и не меняет bits при выключенном флаге.

**Original commit message**

```
The launch check after the native fused expert kernels (ck(cudaGetLastError(), "experts_native")) reports the first error the process has pending, which can come
from any earlier kernel of any stream, so "experts_native: an illegal memory access" names the check and not the kernel.

With STRATA_PF_SYNC_DEBUG=1 every native fused launch of the CUDA K-quant and the generic branch is followed by a stream synchronize, and an error prints the
stage (gate/up or down), the pair, the routed rows, the experts of the batch, the tile and grid, and the memory kind of every blob in the batch.  Off by default:
the default launches are unchanged.  Output bits equal (prefill_expert_paths_bench hash_fused, three K-quant pairs at 17 / 300 / 1,024 / 5,888 / 8,300 tokens,
RTX A4000: base build, this build, this build with the switch on).
```

### `850002823549` — fix(inkling): the engine no longer crashes or hangs at shutdown after QUIT

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `engine/src/inkling2/ink_main.cpp` (+4/-1)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/ink_prefix_mock.cpp` (+26/-2)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/ink_serve.h` (+45/-33)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/test_shared_prefix.py` (+45/-0)

- Related decisions:
  - No direct matrix decision; accepted as upstream baseline.

**Original commit message**

```
The stdin reader of the serve loop (ServeInput) is a detached thread blocked
in getline().  Its mutex, condition variable, queue and EOF flag were members
of an object living in run_serve_loop()'s frame, and the thread captured
`this`.  After QUIT the loop returns and the object is destroyed while the
backend is still being torn down; when the client then closed stdin, the
reader woke on EOF and locked a destroyed mutex ("terminate called after
throwing an instance of 'std::system_error': Invalid argument", or glibc's
tpp.c assertion) or wrote into a freed frame (segmentation fault).  Seen as
about 1 run in 6 on a 5090 and on every build checked by PERF-STEP.

- ServeInput keeps its state in a shared_ptr<State> that the thread owns a
  share of, so it outlives the serve loop.
- With stdin still open exit() never returned (the reader holds the stdio
  lock, the client had to kill the engine): after run_serve_loop the backend
  is released and the process leaves with leave_after_quit() (flush, _Exit).
- ink_prefix_mock: INK_TEST_SLOW_TEARDOWN_MS slows the backend's teardown and
  the teardown overwrites the stack below main as a real one does;
  test_shared_prefix.py QuitShutdown: QUIT then stdin closed at once, closed
  while the backend is torn down (the crash), and left open (the hang).  On the
  base the mock aborts with the system_error above and hangs with stdin open;
  with this change all three pass.

Real engine (z440, RTX A4000, strata-inkling CUDA 13 sm_86, SQ-15, one short
GEN, then STOP + QUIT, stdin closed 0 to 0.25 s later, 40 starts): base 30 of
40 clean (10 x abort: system_error "Invalid argument" or the tpp.c assertion,
all at 0.03 to 0.06 s), this change 40 of 40.  Stdin left open: base hangs (6
of 6 killed after 30 s), this change 6 of 6 exit 0.
```

### `0bd162f43f70` — feat(inkling): --tail-role-token ID, an opt-in prompt checkpoint in front of the trailing thinking-level turn (#7241)

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `engine/src/inkling2/ink_main.cpp` (+3/-0)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/ink_prefix_mock.cpp` (+1/-0)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/ink_serve.h` (+26/-7)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/test_shared_prefix.py` (+59/-1)

- Related decisions:
  - No direct matrix decision; accepted as upstream baseline.

**Original commit message**

```
Control's effort_position "end" puts the level of a request that is not the default in a short <|message_system|> turn at the END of the prompt. With --tail-role-token (the id of <|message_system|>) the serve loop takes the prompt checkpoint one token in front of that turn (prompt_ckpt_position) instead of at the end of the prompt, so a request that changes only the level, or the next turn of the chat, restores the checkpoint and reads only the tail. Off (the default): unchanged, the checkpoint is at prompt_len - 1. ink_prefix_mock + test_shared_prefix.py TailLevel: reuse counts on and off, the tokens on, off and cold are identical.
```

### `5bafbe3bcdb1` — engine(cpu): the 4-row dot kernels are the default from two tokens per expert call, off for one token (#7075, #6552)

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `docs/SETTINGS.md` (+3/-3)
  - `engine/include/strata/quant/dot_x4_policy.hpp` (+34/-5)
  - `engine/src/core/dot_x4_policy_test.cpp` (+29/-9)
  - `engine/src/kernels/cpu/native_expert.cpp` (+4/-4)
  - `terminal/data/settings-register.json` (+2/-2)

- Related decisions:
  - `settings-fork-knobs`: ours — FDEC/DOWN_SUB8 и связанные fork-настройки отсутствуют в upstream delta.
  - `fused-format-support`: ours — Upstream добавляет диагностику, но не нашу поддержку IQ4_XS down и IQ4_NL gate/up.
  - `q36-fa-v2`: ours — Upstream не содержит fork FA tile/map/check/prof контракта.
  - `q36-fdec`: ours — Upstream не содержит fork FDEC decode route.
  - `q36-p4`: ours — Upstream не содержит fork P4 fused prompt route.
  - `q36-memory-staging`: ours — Upstream не содержит fork staging-one и memory controls.
  - `q36-super-chunk`: ours — Upstream не содержит fork layer-major super-chunk route.
  - `down-sub8`: ours — Upstream не содержит fork opt-out для 32-lane down reduction.

**Original commit message**

```
0.2.21 made the dot_x4 kernels of the native CPU expert path opt-in
(STRATA_DOT_X4=1) because a single-thread cold bench showed them slower on a
DRAM-bound row.  #7075 measured the other side (2x RTX 5090, PCIe-streamed
NVFP4, 4 slots): without them warm batched decode lost ~15% and short prompt
reads ~8% against 0.1.66, and STRATA_DOT_X4=1 recovered both, while solo
decode (one token per expert) was better with them off.

The default now follows the call, as the reporter suggested and the reply on
the issue promised: on from kDotX4MinTokens = 2 tokens that share the expert in
one call, off for one token.  STRATA_DOT_X4=1 forces them on for every call,
STRATA_DOT_X4=0 off for every call (exactly the 0.2.21 / 0.2.22 default),
STRATA_NO_DOT_X4 (any value) still wins.  The two forms give the same bits:
dot_x4_test, and cpu_dispatch_dump hashes of every nt 1..8, whole and off-vector
row ranges, both arithmetics, identical for off / default / on / NO_DOT_X4
(zz_x4_proof.sh); pool_determinism passes in all three modes.

dot_x4_policy.hpp: dot_x4_mode() (read once) and dot_x4_enabled(mode, nt);
dot_x4_policy_test covers every case.
```

### `3886fe60a89b` — feat(inkling2-cuda): DFlash 2 on sm_60, sm_61, sm_70 and sm_75 cards (FP32 matrices, dp4a head screen on sm_60)

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_cuda_backend.cu` (+1/-0)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_dflash2.cuh` (+78/-27)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_dflash2_kernels.cuh` (+80/-21)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_dflash2_path.cuh` (+12/-1)

- Related decisions:
  - No direct matrix decision; accepted as upstream baseline.

**Original commit message**

```
DFlash 2 refused below sm_80 ("needs the Q8 unembed screen and an sm_80+ card"). It is the only drafter since 11-26, so a P100, GTX 10, V100 or RTX 20 card ran with no drafter.
It now runs there:

- the BF16 matrices of the checkpoint (fc, K / V, the two convolution kernels, the selector) are held as FP32, the checkpoint's own values (bf16 -> fp32 is exact), and multiplied with
  cuBLAS FP32 on FP32 activations; no BF16 GEMM, no tensor-core mma. The block weights are never BF16 there (Q8, or the Q8 + SF4 mix on a card below 24 GB: the existing dp4a GEMVs).
- the head screen keeps the cuBLAS INT8 GEMM from sm_61; the P100 (sm_60, no INT8 GEMM) has a dp4a kernel with the same tile layout and values (k_df2_screen_gemm).
- the skinny GEMVs are templated on the rows per block: 8 rows of the down projection need 62 KB of shared memory, more than the 48 KB of an sm_60 / sm_61 block, so below sm_80 that
  matrix runs 4 rows per block.
- STRATA_INK_DFLASH2_F32=1 runs the FP32 form on an sm_80+ card (test hook: bf16 form against fp32 form on one card). STRATA_INK_DFLASH2_SCREEN=cublas|kernel picks the screen for A/Bs.
- df2_f32_path() (set by the backend from the card's compute capability) also moves the sizing: 112 -> 224 MiB for the matrices held as FP32.

Proof that the FP32 form is the same drafter (RTX A4000, sm_86, one binary, SQ-20 v2, six real prompts, 2 rounds, STRATA_INK_DFLASH2_F32=1 against the default BF16 form; plain = --draft off):
  accepted / offered draft tokens: prose 134/266 vs 136/264, chat 164/244 vs 150/262, json 208/230 vs 208/230, code 196/228 vs 196/228, tool 186/274 vs 186/286, doc4k 32/62 both
  decode tok/s: prose 42.4 vs 43.4, chat 53.9 vs 49.0, json 52.5 vs 53.1, code 56.6 vs 56.0, tool 51.0 vs 49.7, doc4k 25.4 vs 25.5
  final tokens FP32 vs BF16: identical on prose, json, code, doc4k; chat differs from token 4, tool from token 92. Plain decode equals the FP32 form on all five prompts and the BF16
  form differs from plain on those same two (the engine's batched verify is not bit-identical to M = 1; which side of a near tie a draft lands on changes with the draft's rounding).
On the old cards, V100 / P100 / RTX 2080 Ti (SQ-20 v2, decode tok/s, DFlash 2 vs the shipped DSpark): V100 prose 66.0 vs 47.0, json 130 vs 93, code 118 vs 113; P100 prose 29.8 vs 20.4,
json 35.4 vs 26.6; 2080 Ti prose 16.5 vs 13.4, json 20.0 vs 15.7. A 4K document prompt is slower than DSpark (V100 61 vs 87; the draft step costs 4.9 ms): see phaseA-tests\rel0223\nv-older\RESULTS.md.
```

### `9c313502faa9` — feat(inkling2): GPU picture reader for NVIDIA (BF16 tensor cores, FP32-class accuracy), loaded with the first picture and freed after 120 s idle

- Date: 2026-11-26

- Related decisions:
  - No direct matrix decision; accepted as upstream baseline.

**Original commit message**

```
Inkling pictures were read on the CPU on every NVIDIA card; only HIP, SYCL and Metal had a GPU encoder.
ink_vision_cuda.cuh runs the same 23-layer ViT as the CPU reference: BF16 weights on the card, cuBLAS BF16 GEMM with a two-part (hi + lo) activation,
FP32 attention in chunks of queries, a plain FP32 kernel below sm_80.  Against the CPU reference on 8 pictures (144 to 1,024 tokens, RTX A4000):
max abs error 2e-5 to 1.7e-4, max relative error per row 5e-5 to 4.4e-4, cosine per row >= 0.99999991.  0.04 s at 144 tokens, 0.09 s at 300, 0.70 s at 1,024.
The backend puts the reader on the card with the first picture and gives it back after --vision-idle-s (default 120) without one; its room (weights + a
1,024-token picture) is held back from the experts' budget at start when the reader is planned.  A picture that does not fit is read on the CPU.
strata-inkling-vit-check compares the two readers on real pictures (error, time, memory).
```

### `e62e8448d831` — feat(inkling2): one picture-reader rule for every backend (the Qwen rule): CPU reader under 24 GB, the card's from 24 GB, Mac always GPU, --vision-reader

- Related decisions:

**Original commit message**

```
ink_vision_policy.h holds the rule: NVIDIA and AMD under 23.5 GiB reported read pictures on the CPU so the experts keep the card; 23.5 GiB or more uses the
card's encoder; Metal always the GPU; Intel Arc the CPU unless asked (as Qwen); unified-memory machines apply the same number to the pool they report.
--vision-reader auto|gpu|cpu (and STRATA_INK_VISION_READER) wins, with a note on a small card; --vision-idle-s sets the NVIDIA idle time.
ink_serve.h asks the backend's encoder only when the rule says GPU, prints the choice, and says vision_reader=gpu|cpu|off in the INFO line;
--info says vision_reader=1 so Control passes the flag only to an engine that knows it.  While no request runs the serve loop gives an idle GPU reader back.
HIP and SYCL report the main card's memory alone.  ink_vision_policy_test covers every backend and card size.
```

### `0b493c7d802d` — feat(inkling2-cuda): a pre-quantized DFlash 2 sidecar is expanded to bf16 on cards of 24 GiB and more; batches above 32 slots decode plainly there

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_dflash2.cuh` (+26/-0)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_dflash2_kernels.cuh` (+40/-0)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_dflash2_path.cuh` (+3/-1)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_dspark.cuh` (+1/-1)

- Related decisions:
  - No direct matrix decision; accepted as upstream baseline.

**Original commit message**

```
Measured on an RTX 5090 (SQ-20, fast mode, mixed prompts, against no drafter): with the mix quantized in the GEMV (dp4a) path DFlash 2 won 12.6 percent at 16 slots but lost 3.5 percent at
32 and 9 percent at 64, where bf16 (cuBLAS tensor cores) won 12.4 percent at 32. The card's row says bf16, so a pre-quantized sidecar (format strata-dflash2-q1) is dequantized to bf16 while it
is loaded (k_df2_q8row_to_bf16, k_df2_q4_to_bf16, k_df2_sf4_to_bf16): the download stays 552 MB, the speed is bf16's. On smaller cards the file's own format is used. Above 32 slots on a
32 GB card DFlash 2 still lost 7.5 percent at 64 (bf16), so the drafter takes at most 32 slots there and larger batches decode plainly (measured: -1.5 percent at 48, -1 percent at 64 against
no drafter, the tap capture that remains).

Credit: tjm8874 for the DFlash 2 drafter design and the two kernels adapted from it; weights incoai/Inkling-2-Mini-DFlash2 (Apache-2.0).
```

### `7f6c54dbfd78` — fix(inkling2-cuda): DFlash 2 starts with as many context rings as fit (a 16 GB card in compact mode), instead of all 8 or no drafter

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_dflash2.cuh` (+9/-2)

- Related decisions:
  - No direct matrix decision; accepted as upstream baseline.

**Original commit message**

```
With the mix (331 MiB of weights, 40 MiB more than the 4-bit groups) the 8 start-up rings did not all fit beside the rest of the compact 16 GB plan (RTX 5060 Ti, 64 slots, fast mode):
the drafter did not start and the run had no drafter (216 tok/s, the same as --draft off; with the 4-bit groups it had run at 348). The rings that fit are taken, the slots without one are
marked as not drafting for good (the memory is what the rest of the engine needs) and decode plainly; no ring at all is still a failed start that gives everything back.

Credit: tjm8874 for the DFlash 2 drafter design; weights incoai/Inkling-2-Mini-DFlash2 (Apache-2.0).
```

### `b8435c3f99fb` — sm120: the COMPACT precheck counts what DFlash 2 and the runner really take and keeps a 256 MiB crash margin; the K/V sizing uses the same real numbers

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_runner.cuh` (+48/-4)

- Related decisions:
  - No direct matrix decision; accepted as upstream baseline.

**Original commit message**

```
Measured (RTX 5060 Ti 16 GB, SQ-20 v2, DFlash 2 mix beside the model; RTX 5080 numbers from the SM120-ROWS lane): DFlash 2 takes 432 MiB for 1 slot (331 weights + 15 scratch + a 41 MiB ring + 45 MiB first use),
the workspaces after the K/V take 367 MiB at max_batch 256 (130 + 237, not 130 + 320), and the runner takes 234 MiB between the end of the load and the K/V sizing (1425 -> 1191 MiB free on the 5060 Ti, 1282 -> 1050 on the 5080).
The sizing reserved 492 + 450 MiB, so on both cards it moved ALL device K/V to mapped host memory (7680 -> 0 tokens) and ended with 285 (5060 Ti) / 144 MiB (5080) free.

Precheck (ink_runner.cuh, COMPACT with 1 slot): DSpark's weights are no longer counted unless STRATA_INK_DRAFTER=dspark loads them (the estimate was 306 MiB too high); DFlash 2's real total is added to the
headroom; and the end of the start is predicted (free after the load - runner - DFlash 2, no device K/V) and must leave at least 256 MiB free, else COMPACT refuses with one line and the old path runs. The 5080 (15,558 MiB free)
predicts 147 MiB and refuses; the 5060 Ti default (15,698 MiB free) predicts 288 (285 measured) and runs; a 5060 Ti with 4 or 8 MiB of ballast (15,561 / 15,557 MiB free) refuses. No device K/V floor: whether COMPACT pays on a card is
decided per card row. K/V sizing: DFlash 2 counted as 432 MiB, workspaces as max_batch x 520 KiB + 237 MiB. With it the 5060 Ti default keeps 7808 device K/V tokens (end of the start 161 MiB free, 128 kept by the cut)
instead of 0, and the C1 numbers rise: prose 76.6 -> 82.9, json 140.9 -> 159.3, code 220.1 -> 241.5 tok/s; an 8K prompt prefills at 3864 instead of 1556 tok/s, a 32K prompt at 882 instead of 598
(five-arm table in phaseA-tests/rel0223/dflash2/data/five_arm_5060ti_summary.txt; the old path with DFlash 2 gives 47 / 29 / 79 tok/s and prefill 504 / 446).
```

### `1853290f02be` — inkling2 HIP (RDNA2): 64 x 64 tiled fdot2 GEMM for the dense BF16 projections and tiled fdot2 grouped-MoE kernels for prefill

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `engine/src/inkling2/hip/ink_hip_backend.hip.cpp` (+6/-12)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/hip/ink_hip_codecs.hip.h` (+134/-6)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/hip/ink_hip_gguf_types_test.hip.cpp` (+138/-6)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/hip/ink_hip_rdna2_gemm.hip.h` (+97/-0)

- Related decisions:
  - No direct matrix decision; accepted as upstream baseline.

**Original commit message**

```
gfx103x has no matrix cores. The attention projections went through k_bf16_fdot2_tiled<16, 32> (2 x 2 outputs per thread, element-wise loads, one LDS read per fdot2): 1.0 TFLOPs on
a RX 6700 XT. k_bf16_fdot2_tiled64 (ink_hip_rdna2_gemm.hip.h: 64 x 64 tile per 256 threads, 4 x 4 per thread, 16-byte LDS reads, next tile's global loads in flight) sums every output in the
same order and gives bit-identical results at 9.0 TFLOPs. STRATA_HIP_RDNA2_GEMM=0 keeps the old kernel.

The grouped MoE for prefill on RDNA2 read the weights once per 8 tokens in a GEMV form. k_moe_grouped_w13_situ_wmma and k_moe_grouped_w2_wmma now also have a body for targets
without WMMA (the gathered tokens and the decoded weight tile are staged in LDS as before; 4 tokens x 8 weight rows per thread with fdot2; the WMMA code of RDNA3 / RDNA4 is
untouched). launch_moe_w13_rdna2 / launch_moe_w2_rdna2 use them from 96 tokens in the step (STRATA_HIP_RDNA2_MOE=0 keeps the per-row kernels). The HIP types test has `gemm` (bit-identity and
TFLOPs) and `moe` (tiles against the per-row kernels on a synthetic GGUF expert) modes.
```

### `918879984081` — sm120 table: measured rows for the RTX 5080, 5070 Ti, 5070 (12 GB), 5060 (8 GB) and the RTX PRO 6000 / 5000 / 4500 / 4000

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `engine/include/strata/core/ink_sm120_table.hpp` (+38/-25)
  - `engine/tests/core/ink_sm120_table_test.cpp` (+20/-6)

- Related decisions:
  - No direct matrix decision; accepted as upstream baseline.

**Original commit message**

```
Measured on rented Vast boxes and l620 (SQ-20 v2, f-sm120-rows / f-0223-int builds, C1 on 6 prompts, C4, C8, 4K and 32K prefill and decode, 2-3 rounds), FASTQ off against fast.
- RTX PRO 6000 / 5000 / 4500 / 4000: the 5090 set plus TC_ATTN (kSm120ProFast). With the 5090 row alone these cards decode slower than off at 32K (PRO 5000 72 GB: 150 off, 111 fast;
  with TC_ATTN 186); the 4K numbers and the prefill do not move; teacher-forced KL of fast + TC_ATTN against off equals the fast set's (0.006-0.011). Default on for the first three
  (FASTQ equal or better); the 4000 stays off until its FASTQ is in.
- RTX 5070 12 GB and RTX 5060 8 GB: COMPACT_HYBRID (kSm120HybridFast: compact trunk, FP16 KV, tensor-core small-M kernels; no PACK / PREFILL / PLD / CORE_GRAPH, which made these cards slower).
  The 5070 is default on (FASTQ within noise, KL at the engine floor); the 5060 stays off until its FASTQ is in.
- RTX 5080 and 5070 Ti (16 GB): measured, fast = compact with the Bk128 MoE variant (+8% steps/s), default OFF: 32K prefill and decode lose with the K/V in host memory.
- the table test is updated (10 measured rows) and checks the new defaults.
```

### `402cd017e492` — engine: the ONE drafter on/off rule: residency first at load, plain steps by context on hybrid cards

- Date: 2026-11-26
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `engine/CMakeLists.txt` (+4/-0)
  - `engine/include/strata/core/ink_draft_rule.hpp` (+66/-0)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_cuda_backend.cu` (+45/-1)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/cuda/ink_dflash2_path.cuh` (+15/-2)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/ink_backend.h` (+1/-0)
  - `engine/src/inkling2/ink_main.cpp` (+1/-0)
  - `engine/tests/core/ink_draft_rule_test.cpp` (+59/-0)
  - `terminal/data/settings-register.json` (+129/-101)

- Related decisions:
  - `cmake-upstream-build`: upstream — Берём version 0.1.70, symbols, sm120/draft-rule tests и dot-x4 policy; fork parity tests сохраняются отдельно.

**Original commit message**

```
strata/core/ink_draft_rule.hpp (host-only, unit-tested by ink_draft_rule_test) states two principles for every backend:
1. Residency first. A default drafter is not loaded when its weights and rings would move the model from full residency (every expert in VRAM) to a hybrid load, or cut the
   expert tier by 10% or more. One line is printed, with the sentence start the HIP backend uses ("DFlash 2 not loaded: it would move part of the model off the card (the experts
   need X GiB, Y GiB fit with the drafter's Z MiB, W GiB without it) ... `--draft on` loads it anyway."). `--draft on` (BackendOpts::draft_forced), STRATA_INK_DFLASH2_FORCE=1 and
   STRATA_INK_DRAFTER=dflash2 ask for it by name and are never vetoed; `--draft off` stays off.
2. Plain steps by context. On a hybrid card (experts in host memory) a step from kDraftPlainCtxHybrid tokens of context on is a plain step, per step, so it follows every request.
   With all experts resident the existing window governor decides alone. The rule never turns drafting on.
CUDA: the backend compares the load plan with and without the drafter against the free VRAM, the trunk and the expert section (the COMPACT path keeps its own accounting) and sets
df2_residency_veto(), which df2_requested() reads. Tested on an RTX PRO 4000 24 GB with SQ-20 and a VRAM ballast: ballast 2600 MiB keeps full residency with DFlash 2 loaded (no line);
3000 MiB is the flip (3072 experts resident without DFlash 2, 3034 with it): the line is printed and DFlash 2 stays out, `--draft on` loads it (38 experts to host); 3400 and 3800 MiB
(hybrid either way, tier cut below 10%) load it. BackendOpts::draft_forced and its `--draft` parsing are the same lines the HIP backend adds.
```

### `38db450a4783` — test(snapshot): the engine stub carries --prompt-cache-spread, as a current engine does; the golden files stay

- Date: 2026-11-27
- Author: Niko1221
- Files:
  - `terminal/tests/snapshot/world.py` (+3/-1)

- Related decisions:
  - No direct matrix decision; accepted as upstream baseline.

**Original commit message**

```
tests/snapshot was red on the release base (87 failing subtests, every machine x model set). Cause, found by regenerating the golden files on the
f-0223-int tip and reading the diff: the ONLY differences were the option --prompt-cache-spread leaving the engine's argv and one note line
("This engine does not support spreading prompt cache checkpoints ..."). serve.engine_args drops that option for an engine program whose file does not
contain the string (#6870, 2026-11-23, already in v0.2.22), and the snapshot world's engine STUB (tests/snapshot/world.py ENGINE_STUB, "the strings a
current engine carries") never had it, so the test has been red since #6870. A current engine does carry the option (the golden files of
00810bdd9 are right), so the stub was stale, not the lineup's golden files: no lineup, DFlash 2, flag or setting change shows up in them.

The stub gets the string; nothing in golden/ changes. tests/snapshot: 64 passed, 548 subtests (p2006, golden untouched).
```
